That’s what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I’ve been trying not to make too big a deal out of it, but it’s like the color has sort of drained out of everything.\n\nEven when the house is busy and everyone’s around, I just feel… hopeless, I guess. I’ll go hide in the laundry room and cry because I don’t want anyone to see me like that. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when your mood was unusually high, energized, or irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Um… maybe, yes. I almost don’t want to call it “high,” because that sounds strange, but earlier this month I had a stretch where I felt really… electrically upbeat, like more energized than normal.\n\nAnd then it wasn’t just positive. I was snapping at people over the smallest things — like if a medication cup was misplaced, or the morning schedule got changed. I’d hear myself and think, why am I reacting like that? It didn’t really feel like me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: About that more energized and irritable stretch earlier this month, how many days did it last?\n\nPatient: I think it was… several days. I don’t want to exaggerate it. Earlier this month there was that stretch where I felt almost electrically upbeat, and then I was snapping at everyone over tiny little things — like the medication cup being out of place or the morning schedule changing.\n\nIt didn’t feel like my usual self. It just didn’t feel like my normal way of reacting. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: During that energized stretch, did you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I’d be up before sunrise and feel really sharp, like I was sure I didn’t actually need more sleep. I know that probably sounds concerning… but that is what it was like.\n\nDoctor: During that energized stretch, were you more active or getting more done than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… I was definitely more active, though I kept telling myself I was just being productive.\n\nAfter work I couldn’t really sit still. I’d be pacing around the house, measuring rooms, making lists, reorganizing cabinets—things like that. I’m trying to answer that carefully — I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: During that energized stretch, were you talking more than usual or felt hard to stop once you got going?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think that was a major part of it.\n\nI mean, I may have talked a bit more if I was excited about something, but I don’t remember people telling me I was talking nonstop or anything like that. I’d be pacing around the house, measuring rooms, making lists, reorganizing cabinets—things like that. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During that energized stretch, did you feel unusually confident or like you had special abilities that were beyond your usual self?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a “special abilities” way, anyway.\n\nI may have felt more sure of myself than usual, like my ideas were good and I could handle things, but I don’t remember thinking I had powers or anything beyond normal. I’m trying to answer that carefully — I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: During that energized stretch, were you talking more than usual or felt hard to stop once you got going?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think that was a major part of it.\n\nI mean, I may have talked a bit more if I was excited about something, but I don’t remember people telling me I was talking nonstop or anything like that. I’m trying hard to cover and double-check myself, but it’s been affecting me.\n\nAnd socially I’ve been avoiding friends more, because I’m afraid I’ll either snap at someone or just… fall apart in front of them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During that energized and irritable stretch, were you hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn’t hospitalized for it.\n\nI mean… it was bad, and I know it was affecting things, but it didn’t get to the point where I was admitted anywhere. I almost don’t want to call it “high,” because that sounds strange, but earlier this month I had a stretch where I felt really… electrically upbeat, like more energized than normal.\n\nAnd then it wasn’t just positive. I was snapping at people over the smallest things — like if a medication cup was misplaced, or the morning schedule got changed. I’d hear myself and think, why am I reacting like that? It didn’t really feel like me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: About that more energized and irritable stretch earlier this month, how many days did it last?\n\nPatient: I think it was… several days. I don’t want to exaggerate it. Earlier this month there was that stretch where I felt almost electrically upbeat, and then I was snapping at everyone over tiny little things — like the medication cup being out of place or the morning schedule changing.\n\nIt didn’t feel like my usual self. I’m sorry, is “several days” enough of an answer?\n\nDoctor: Did that energized and irritable stretch last a full week or longer?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it was a full week, no… at least I don’t want to say that if I’m not completely sure.\n\nIt was earlier this month, and it was definitely several days where I felt almost electrically upbeat, and then I’d snap at people over really small things — like the medication cup being moved or the morning schedule changing. It just didn’t feel like my normal way of reacting. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: During that energized stretch, did you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I was sleeping a lot less than I normally would — maybe around three hours a night.\n\nThe part that feels odd to admit is that I wasn’t waking up exhausted. I’d be up before sunrise and feel really sharp, like I was sure I didn’t actually need more sleep. I know that probably sounds concerning… but that is what it was like.\n\nDoctor: During that energized stretch, were you more active or getting more done than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… I was definitely more active, though I kept telling myself I was just being productive.\n\nAfter work I couldn’t really sit still. I’d be pacing around the house, measuring rooms, making lists, reorganizing cabinets—things like that. And I’d keep going long after everyone else had gone to bed.\n\nIt sounds a little much when I say it out loud, but at the time it felt like I was finally getting things done. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During that energized stretch, did you feel unusually confident or like you had special abilities that were beyond your usual self?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a “special abilities” way, anyway.\n\nI may have felt more sure of myself than usual, like my ideas were good and I could handle things, but I don’t remember thinking I had powers or anything beyond normal. I’m trying to answer that carefully — I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: During that energized stretch, were you talking more than usual or felt hard to stop once you got going?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think that was a major part of it.\n\nI mean, I may have talked a bit more if I was excited about something, but I don’t remember people telling me I was talking nonstop or anything like that. I wasn’t, as far as I know, unable to stop once I started.\n\nIf it helps, I can try to think of specific examples, but nothing is really standing out to me on that one.\n\nDoctor: During that energized stretch, did you do anything more risky or impulsive than usual?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little… I mean, I wouldn’t say I was doing anything dangerous exactly, but I did spend money more impulsively than I normally would.\n\nI bought monitoring equipment, ramps, bedding, and a bunch of supplies because at the time it felt really urgent and necessary, like I had to take care of it right then. And I told myself it was practical, not reckless.\n\nBut afterward, when I looked at the credit card, I realized I’d put charges on there that we really can’t cover. I feel embarrassed saying that… but yes, I guess that was more impulsive than usual.\n\nDoctor: During that energized stretch, did it cause serious problems in your life or at work?\n\nPatient: It did, yes… though I hate saying that because I keep wanting to explain it away.\n\nAt work, at the clinic, I’ve started making mistakes that I normally wouldn’t make, and that really scares me. I’m trying hard to cover and double-check myself, but it’s been affecting me.\n\nAnd socially I’ve been avoiding friends more, because I’m afraid I’ll either snap at someone or just… fall apart in front of them. At home, my husband has had to take over more than I like to admit — bills, meals, and just sort of keeping an eye on me when my thoughts get dark.\n\nSo… yes, I guess it’s caused serious problems. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During that energized and irritable stretch, were you hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn’t hospitalized for it.\n\nI mean… it was bad, and I know it was affecting things, but it didn’t get to the point where I was admitted anywhere. At least, not for that stretch. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another episode in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times before where I felt low or overwhelmed, but I don’t know if I’d call them episodes the way you mean. I may have just pushed through and told myself it was stress or being tired.\n\nSo… maybe, but I’m not sure. I don’t want to be dramatic, and I’m not sure I’m saying this the right way, but… I keep thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd sometimes, driving home from the clinic, I catch myself wondering what would happen if I just… didn’t turn the wheel. That really scared me, so I thought I should probably talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Are you wanting to kill yourself right now?\n\nPatient: Right now… I don’t think I’m trying to, no. I’ve been trying not to make too big a deal out of it, but it’s like the color has sort of drained out of everything.\n\nEven when the house is busy and everyone’s around, I just feel… hopeless, I guess. I’m trying to answer that carefully — I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: During that energized stretch, were you talking more than usual or felt hard to stop once you got going?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think that was a major part of it.\n\nI mean, I may have talked a bit more if I was excited about something, but I don’t remember people telling me I was talking nonstop or anything like that.
